- **batch-effect-quantification-across-layers**: Use when a trained DenseNet121 (DeepMSProfiler ensemble) is available on raw LC-MS .npy data and you need to measure, layer by layer, whether hidden representations shed batch/hospital variation while retaining disease-class information. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/batch-effect-quantification-across-layers))
- **batch-effect-quantification-ilisi**: Use when comparing batch-correction methods (e.g., BATMAN, Seurat V3.0, MNNcorrect, Scanorama) on integrated scRNA-seq datasets and you need a numeric mixing-vs-structure tradeoff score such as mean iLISI and mean 50-RNN. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/batch-effect-quantification-ilisi))
- **batch-effect-quantification-scoring**: Use when comparing imputed vs. raw scRNA-Seq matrices for batch correction on datasets with known batch and cell-type annotations (e.g., Villani PBMC GSE80171, Baron pancreatic islet) and numeric batch-merging scores are needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/batch-effect-quantification-scoring))
- **batch-effect-quantification-via-kl-divergence**: Use when per-cell batch labels and a low-dimensional representation (e.g., DESC's X_Embeded_z in adata.obsm) or derived quantities such as Monocle3 pseudotime are available, and you need a numeric, distribution-level measure of whether batches are well mixed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/batch-effect-quantification-via-kl-divergence))
- **batch-effect-removal-across-protocols**: Use when combining scRNA-seq count matrices generated by different library-preparation protocols or batches, and the goal is cell-type clustering with batch effect removed in a single joint analysis (measured by ARI and cell-type classification accuracy). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/batch-effect-removal-across-protocols))
- **batch-effect-removal-cross-assay**: Use when integrating scRNA-seq and ST count matrices measured on the same shared gene space from different experiments or patients, and a domain-invariant latent feature is needed before spatial projection or joint clustering. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/batch-effect-removal-cross-assay))
- **batch-effect-removal-cross-dataset-integration**: Use when combining two scRNA-Seq datasets generated in different labs/protocols (e.g., Hockley GSE102962 target with Usoskin GSE59739 source) prior to clustering, and you want to test whether batch correction improves cluster recovery. - **batch-effect-removal-evaluation**: Use when you have metabolomics abundance data from multiple plates/batches over an extended acquisition period and need to compare normalization methods on unwanted-variation removal, replicate variability, and retention of biological signal (e.g. disease-status classification). - **batch-effect-removal-evaluation**: Use when assessing whether a scRNA-seq clustering method removes batch effects: you have count matrices with multiple batch metadata fields (e.g., donor, region, sample IDs) and reference cell-type labels to score with ARI.
